\displaystyle\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta)=\mathbb{E}_{x}[(\Phi^{{}^{\prime}}(x^{T}\theta)-\Phi^{{}^{\prime}}(x^{T}\theta^{*}))x].

Thus, for
h\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, we have

\displaystyle h^{T}\nabla^{2}\mathcal{R}(\theta)h=\mathbb{E}_{x}[\Phi^{\prime\prime}(x^{T}\theta)(h^{T}x)^{2}].

Since
x^{T}\theta\leq L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2}
for all
\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}\left(||\theta^{*}||_{2}\right)
, and since
\Phi^{\prime\prime}
is even and non-decreasing on
(-\infty,0]
and non-increasing on
[0,\infty)
, we have
\Phi^{\prime\prime}(x^{T}\theta)\geq\Phi^{\prime\prime}\left(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2}\right)>0
, for all
\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle h^{T}\nabla^{2}\mathcal{R}(\theta)h

\displaystyle\geq\Phi^{\prime\prime}\left(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2}\right)h^{T}\mathbb{E}[xx^{T}]h=\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2})h^{T}\Sigma h

\displaystyle\geq\Phi^{\prime\prime}\left(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2}\right)\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)||h||_{2}^{2}>0.

Hence,
\mathcal{R}(\theta)
is
\Phi^{\prime\prime}\left(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2}\right)\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)
-strongly convex over
\mathbb{B}_{2}\left(||\theta^{*}||_{2}\right)
. Also, since
\Phi
is convex over
\mathbb{R}
and
\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta^{*})=0
, the function
\mathcal{R}
is minimized over
\mathbb{B}_{2}\left(||\theta^{*}||_{2}\right)
at
\theta^{*}
. Hence, for all
\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}\left(||\theta^{*}||_{2}\right)
, and thus for all
\theta\in\mathcal{C}
since
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\mathbb{B}_{2}\left(||\theta^{*}||_{2}\right)
, we have by strong convexity that

\displaystyle||\mathbb{E}[\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,z)]||_{2}

\displaystyle=||\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta)||_{2}=||\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta)-\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta^{*})||_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\Phi^{\prime\prime}\left(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2}\right)\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)}{2}||\theta-\theta^{*}||_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\Phi^{\prime\prime}\left(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2}\right)\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)}{2}\left(||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D\right)>0,

since
\theta^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}\setminus\mathcal{C}
, so there is a strict separation between
\theta^{*}
and
\mathcal{C}
.

Now, for all
i\in[n]
and
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, recall that
\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,z_{i})=(\Phi^{\prime}(x_{i}^{T}\theta)-y_{i})x_{i}
. Also, for
h\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, we have
|(\Phi^{\prime}(x_{i}^{T}\theta)-y_{i})x_{i}^{T}h|\leq(K_{\Phi^{\prime}}+K_{y})L_{x}||h||_{2}
, so clearly,

\displaystyle(\Phi^{\prime}(x_{i}^{T}\theta)-y_{i})x_{i}-\mathbb{E}[(\Phi^{\prime}(x_{i}^{T}\theta)-y_{i})x_{i}]\in\mathcal{G}\left((K_{\Phi^{\prime}}+K_{y})^{2}L_{x}^{2}\right),\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,z_{i})-\mathbb{E}[\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,z_{1})]=\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,z_{1})]\in\mathcal{G}\left(\frac{(K_{\Phi^{\prime}}+K_{y})^{2}L_{x}^{2}}{n}\right).

Hence, by Lemma
15
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,z_{i})-\mathbb{E}[\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,z_{1})]\right\|_{2}\geq t\right)\leq 4^{p}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{8s_{n}^{2}}},\quad\forall\ t\geq 0\ \mbox{and}\ \theta\in\mathbb{R}^{p},

(18)

with
s_{n}^{2}=\frac{(K_{\Phi^{\prime}}+K_{y})^{2}L_{x}^{2}}{n}
. Now take
\theta\in\mathcal{C}
and let
Z_{\theta}=\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,z_{i})-\mathbb{E}[\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,z_{1})]\right\|_{2}
. Note that, since
\mathcal{L}(\theta,z)
is
K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}L_{x}^{2}
-smooth over
\mathbb{R}^{p}
for all
z\in\mathcal{E}
, the function
Z_{\theta}
is
2K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}L_{x}^{2}
-Lipschitz over
\mathbb{R}^{p}
. Now we use a covering argument to obtain a concentration result on
\mathop{\sup}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}Z_{\theta}
. Let
t\geq 0
and
\upsilon=\frac{t}{4K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}L_{x}^{2}}
. Take a
\upsilon
-cover
\{\theta^{1},\dots,\theta^{N_{\upsilon}}\}
of
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{B}_{2}(D)
with covering number
N_{\upsilon}:=N(\upsilon,\mathcal{C},||\cdot||_{2})
. Then, for
\theta\in\mathcal{C}
, there is some
k\in[N_{\upsilon}]
such that
||\theta-\theta^{k}||_{2}\leq\upsilon
, and by the Lipschitz property, we have
|Z_{\theta}-Z_{\theta^{k}}|\leq 2K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}L_{x}^{2}\upsilon
. So, if
Z_{\theta}\geq t
, we have
Z_{\theta^{k}}\geq t-2K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}L_{x}^{2}\upsilon=\frac{t}{2}
, since
Z_{\theta},Z_{\theta^{k}}\geq 0
. Hence, by Lemma
15
and Lemma
22
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathop{\sup}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}Z_{\theta}\geq t\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\mathop{\sup}\limits_{k\in[N_{\upsilon}]}Z_{\theta^{k}}\geq\frac{t}{2}\right)\leq\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\upsilon}}\mathbb{P}\left(Z_{\theta^{k}}\geq\frac{t}{2}\right)\leq\left(1+\frac{2D}{\upsilon}\right)^{p}4^{p}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{32s_{n}^{2}}}

\displaystyle=\left(1+\frac{8K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}L_{x}^{2}D}{t}\right)^{p}4^{p}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{32s_{n}^{2}}}\leq\left(\frac{16K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}L_{x}^{2}D}{t}\right)^{p}4^{p}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{32s_{n}^{2}}},

for
t\leq 8K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}L_{x}^{2}D
. Since
D\leq||\theta^{*}||_{2}\asymp 1
, we have absolute constants
C_{2}
and
C_{3}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathop{\sup}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}Z_{\theta}\geq t\right)\leq\frac{C_{2}}{t^{p}}4^{p}e^{-\frac{nt^{2}}{C_{3}}},

and by rescaling
t
with
4C_{2}^{1/p}t
, since
p
is of constant order, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathop{\sup}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}Z_{\theta}\geq t\right)\leq\frac{1}{t^{p}}e^{-\frac{nt^{2}}{C_{4}}},

for
t\leq C_{5}
, with absolute constants
C_{4}
and
C_{5}
. Fix
\zeta\in(0,1)
. Thus, we want
t\leq C_{5}
and
\frac{1}{t^{p}}e^{-\frac{nt^{2}}{C_{4}}}\leq\frac{\zeta}{2}
, or equivalently,
t^{2}+\frac{pC_{4}}{n}\log(t)\geq\frac{C_{4}}{n}\log(2/\zeta)
. Pick
t=\sqrt{\frac{C_{4}\log(2/\zeta)}{n}}+\frac{1}{n^{q}}
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2q}}+\frac{2}{n^{q}}\sqrt{\frac{C_{4}\log(2/\zeta)}{n}}+\frac{pC_{4}}{n}\log\left(\sqrt{\frac{C_{4}\log(2/\zeta)}{n}}+\frac{1}{n^{q}}\right)\geq 0,

since if we pick
n
greater than an absolute constant, the LHS scales like
\frac{1}{n^{2q}}+\frac{1}{n^{q+\frac{1}{2}}}-\frac{C^{\prime\prime}_{1}\log(n)}{n}
, which is greater than
0
, since
q<\frac{1}{2}
and
C^{\prime\prime}_{1}
is some absolute constant. Thus, there is an absolute constant
C^{\prime}_{1}
such that for
n\geq C^{\prime}_{1}
,
the required conditions are satisfied and we have
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{1})\geq 1-\frac{\zeta}{2}
, with

\displaystyle\Omega_{1}=\left\{\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,z_{i})-\mathbb{E}[\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,z_{1})]\right\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}\log(2/\zeta)}{n}}+\frac{1}{n^{q}},\quad\forall\theta\in\mathcal{C}\right\}.

Shifting our attention to Algorithm
3
, we have at step
t
that

\displaystyle v_{t}^{T}(\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta_{t},\mathcal{D}_{n})+\xi_{t})\leq v^{T}(\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta_{t},\mathcal{D}_{n})+\xi_{t}),\quad\forall v\in\mathcal{C},

so

\displaystyle v_{t}^{T}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta_{t},\mathcal{D}_{n})

\displaystyle\leq v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta_{t},\mathcal{D}_{n})+(v-v_{t})^{T}\xi_{t}

\displaystyle\leq v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta_{t},\mathcal{D}_{n})+2D||\xi_{t}||_{2},\quad\forall v\in\mathcal{C}.

By Lemma
15
, for

\displaystyle\Omega_{2}=\left\{||\xi_{t}||_{2}\leq\sqrt{8\left(\frac{8L_{2}}{n}\right)^{2}\frac{T}{\epsilon^{2}}\log\left(\frac{5T}{2\delta}\right)\log\left(\frac{2}{\delta}\right)\log(4^{p}T/\zeta)},\quad\forall t\in[T]\right\},

we have
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{2})\geq 1-\frac{\zeta}{2}
. Let us work on
\Omega=\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}
, with
\mathbb{P}(\Omega)\geq 1-\zeta
. On
\Omega
, we have

\displaystyle||\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})||_{2}\geq\frac{\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2})\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)}{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D)-\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}\log(2/\zeta)}{n}}-\frac{1}{n^{q}}\geq r>0

by the triangle inequality, so we have the desired lower bound on
||\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})||_{2}
for all
\theta\in\mathcal{C}
, with high probability. Next, note that

\displaystyle v_{t}^{T}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta_{t},\mathcal{D}_{n})

\displaystyle\leq v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta_{t},\mathcal{D}_{n})

\displaystyle\quad+2D\sqrt{8\left(\frac{8L_{2}}{n}\right)^{2}\frac{T}{\epsilon^{2}}\log\left(\frac{5T}{2\delta}\right)\log\left(\frac{2}{\delta}\right)\log\left(\frac{4^{p}T}{\zeta}\right)},\quad\forall v\in\mathcal{C},

where we used the fact that
2D=\mathop{\sup}\limits_{x,y\in\mathcal{C}}||x-y||_{2}
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle v_{t}^{T}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta_{t},\mathcal{D}_{n})

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\min}\limits_{v\in\mathcal{C}}v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta_{t},\mathcal{D}_{n})

\displaystyle\quad+2D\sqrt{8\left(\frac{8L_{2}}{n}\right)^{2}\frac{T}{\epsilon^{2}}\log\left(\frac{5T}{2\delta}\right)\log\left(\frac{2}{\delta}\right)\log\left(\frac{4^{p}T}{\zeta}\right)}.

So on
\Omega
, we are in the context of Algorithm
1
and Theorem
1
, with

\displaystyle\Delta=2D\sqrt{8\left(\frac{8L_{2}}{n}\right)^{2}\frac{T}{\epsilon^{2}}\log(5T/2\delta)\log(2/\delta)\log(4^{p}T/\zeta)}.

Note also that
\mathcal{C}
is compact and
\alpha_{\mathcal{C}}
-strongly convex by Lemma
5
, and that we established the smoothness condition and the lower bound on the
\ell_{2}
-norm of the gradient of the empirical risk. Therefore, with probability at least
1-\zeta
, we have for
\eta=\min\left\{1,\frac{\alpha_{\mathcal{C}}r}{4K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}L_{x}^{2}}\right\}
and
c=\max\left\{\frac{1}{2},1-\frac{\alpha_{\mathcal{C}}r}{8K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}L_{x}^{2}}\right\}
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\theta_{T},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})

\displaystyle\leq h_{0}c^{T}+\frac{3\Delta\eta}{2(1-c)}

\displaystyle=h_{0}c^{T}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{3\eta}{(1-c)}D\sqrt{8\left(\frac{8L_{2}}{n}\right)^{2}\frac{T}{\epsilon^{2}}\log\left(\frac{5T}{2\delta}\right)\log\left(\frac{2}{\delta}\right)\log\left(\frac{4^{p}T}{\zeta}\right)}.

Observe that
L_{2}=(K_{\Phi^{\prime}}+K_{y})L_{x}\asymp 1
,
h_{0}\leq 2L_{2}D\asymp 1
by the Lipschitz property. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\theta_{T},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})\lesssim\frac{1}{n}+\frac{\eta\log\left(\log_{1/c}(n)/\delta\right)\sqrt{\log_{1/c}(n)\log\left(\log_{1/c}(n)/\zeta\right)}}{(1-c)n\epsilon},

with probability at least
1-\zeta
, as required.

Note that we needed the
L_{2}
-Lipschitz condition to hold for all datasets
\{(x_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
in
\mathcal{E}
, not just for the data drawn i.i.d. from the GLM. This is because we need
\theta_{T}
to be private, and this is the case if the empirical risk is
L_{2}
-Lipschitz in
\theta
for
arbitrary
data.

D.1.4
Proof of Theorem
5

We will prove a more general statement. Let
\zeta\in(0,1/3)
and
q<\frac{1}{2}
. Assuming
||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{q}}
, there are absolute constants
C^{\prime}_{1},C_{1},C_{2}
, and
C_{3}
such that for
n>\max\left\{C_{2}\log^{\frac{1}{1-2q}}(2/\zeta),C^{\prime}_{1}\right\}
,
D\leq||\theta^{*}||_{2}-\frac{C_{3}}{n^{q}}
, and

\displaystyle r=\frac{1}{n^{q}}-\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}\log(2/\zeta)}{n}},

we have with probability at least
1-3\zeta
that Algorithm
3
with
T=\log_{1/c}(n)
returns
\theta_{T}
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\theta_{T},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})\lesssim\log(n/\delta)\sqrt{\log(n)\log(n/\zeta)}\left(\frac{1}{{n^{1-q/2}\epsilon}}+\frac{1}{n^{q+\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{1}{n^{2q}}\right).

(19)

All the other quantities are as in the theorem hypothesis. Once we prove this, we will optimize the upper bound on the excess empirical risk over
q<\frac{1}{2}
to obtain the desired result.

Let
C_{3}=\frac{4}{\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2})\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)}
. By assumption, we have
||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D\geq\frac{4}{\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2})\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)n^{q}}
. By Theorem
4
, there exist absolute constants
C^{\prime}_{1}
and
C_{1}
such that for
n\geq C^{\prime}_{1}
,
r=\frac{1}{n^{q}}-\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}\log(2/\zeta)}{n}}
, and
T=\log_{1/c}(n)
, Algorithm
3
returns
\theta_{T}
such that with probability at least
1-\zeta
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\theta_{T},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})\lesssim\frac{1}{n}+\frac{\eta\log\left(\log_{1/c}(n)/\delta\right)\sqrt{\log_{1/c}(n)\log\left(\log_{1/c}(n)/\zeta\right)}}{(1-c)n\epsilon}.

This is because, firstly,

\displaystyle r

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{q}}-\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}\log(2/\zeta)}{n}}\leq\frac{||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D}{C_{3}}-\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}\log(2/\zeta)}{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2})\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)}{4}(||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D)-\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}\log(2/\zeta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2})\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)}{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D)-\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}\log(2/\zeta)}{n}}-\frac{1}{n^{q}},

where in both inequalities, we used the fact that
||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D\geq\frac{C_{3}}{n^{q}}
. Secondly, for
C_{2}=(4C_{1})^{\frac{1}{1-2q}}
, we have
n>C_{2}\log^{\frac{1}{1-2q}}(2/\zeta)
, so
r>\frac{1}{2n^{q}}
. Implicitly,
r>0
, hence we can use Theorem
4
with
r
as above. Also, in the proof of Theorem
4
, we showed that
\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})
is
L_{2}
-Lipschitz and
K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}L_{x}^{2}
-smooth, and on an event
\Omega
which occurs with probability at least
1-\zeta
, we have
||\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})||_{2}>r
for all
\theta\in\mathcal{C}
. Here,
L_{2}=(K_{y}+K_{\Phi^{\prime}})L_{x}
and
\eta=\min\left\{1,\frac{\alpha_{\mathcal{C}}r}{4K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}L_{x}^{2}}\right\}
. Moreover,
r\asymp\frac{1}{n^{q}}
, since
\frac{1}{2n^{q}}<r<\frac{1}{n^{q}}
, implying that
\eta,1-c\asymp\frac{1}{n^{q}}
. Therefore, since
0<\epsilon\lesssim 1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\theta_{T},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n}+\frac{n^{q/2}\log(n/\delta)}{n\epsilon}\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)\log(n/\zeta)}{n^{q}\log\left(\frac{1}{1-\frac{1}{n^{q}}}\right)}}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{\log(n/\delta)\sqrt{\log(n)\log(n/\zeta)}}{n^{1-q/2}\epsilon},

(20)

where we used the facts that
\log(\log_{1/c}(n)/\delta)\lesssim\log(n/\delta)
and
n^{q}\log\left(\frac{1}{1-\frac{1}{n^{q}}}\right)\asymp 1
in the above calculations. To reiterate for clarity, for
C^{\prime}_{1}
and
C_{1}
as in Theorem
4
,
C_{2}=(4C_{1})^{\frac{1}{1-2q}},C_{3}=\frac{4}{\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2})\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)}
,
n>\max\left\{C_{2}\log^{\frac{1}{1-2q}}(2/\zeta),C^{\prime}_{1}\right\}
,
||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D\geq\frac{C_{3}}{n^{q}}
,
r=\frac{1}{n^{q}}-\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}\log(2/\zeta)}{n}}
, and
T=\log_{1/c}(n)
, Algorithm
3
returns
\theta_{T}
such that on
\Omega
we have inequality (
D.1.4
), with
\mathbb{P}(\Omega)\geq 1-\zeta
. On
\Omega
, we then have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\theta_{T},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}(\theta_{T},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})

\displaystyle\quad+\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log(n/\delta)\sqrt{\log(n)\log(n/\zeta)}}{n^{1-q/2}\epsilon}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\log(n/\delta)\sqrt{\log(n)\log(n/\zeta)}}{n^{1-q/2}\epsilon}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathcal{L}(\theta_{B,n},\mathcal{D}_{n}),

(21)

where
\theta_{B,n}
is any minimizer of
\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})
over
\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})
. Note that
\theta_{B,n}
exists since
\mathcal{L}
is continuous and
\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})
is compact. Now define
\mathcal{A}:[0,\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
as
\mathcal{A}(\lambda)=\mathcal{L}(\lambda\theta_{B,n},\mathcal{D}_{n})
. Note that
\mathcal{A}
is continuous and
\mathcal{A}(1)=\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})
. Also,
\mathcal{A}(0)=\mathcal{L}(0,\mathcal{D}_{n})\geq\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})
, since
0\in\mathcal{C}
. Moreover, we have

\mathcal{A}(0)\geq\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})\geq\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})=\mathcal{A}(1).

Thus, by the Intermediate Value Theorem, there exists
\lambda_{n}\in[0,1]
such that
\mathcal{A}(\lambda_{n})=\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\theta_{T},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log(n/\delta)\sqrt{\log(n)\log(n/\zeta)}}{n^{1-q/2}\epsilon}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{L}(\lambda_{n}\theta_{B,n},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathcal{L}(\theta_{B,n},\mathcal{D}_{n}).

Now, we have a few cases:

1.

Case
1
:
\theta_{B,n}
is at the boundary of
\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})
. If
\lambda_{n}\theta_{B,n}
is at the boundary of
\mathcal{C}
, then

||\lambda_{n}\theta_{B,n}-\theta_{B,n}||_{2}=||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D\asymp\frac{1}{n^{q}}.

Now suppose
\lambda_{n}\theta_{B,n}
is in the interior of
\mathcal{C}
. Recall that
\mathcal{L}(\lambda_{n}\theta_{B,n},\mathcal{D}_{n})=\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})
. Since
\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})
is convex in
\theta
, we must then have
\nabla\mathcal{L}(\lambda_{n}\theta_{B,n},\mathcal{D}_{n})=0
, so
\lambda_{n}\theta_{B,n}
is a global minizer of
\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})
. Hence, we have
\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})\leq\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})
, so
\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})=0
. If
\lambda_{n}\theta_{B,n}
is outside
\mathcal{C}
, then

||\lambda_{n}\theta_{B,n}-\theta_{B,n}||_{2}\leq||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{q}}.

2.

Case
2
:
\theta_{B,n}
is in the interior of
\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})
. If
\lambda_{n}\theta_{B,n}
is at the boundary of
\mathcal{C}
, then

||\lambda_{n}\theta_{B,n}-\theta_{B,n}||_{2}\leq||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D\asymp\frac{1}{n^{q}}.

Suppose now that
\lambda_{n}\theta_{B,n}
is in the interior of
\mathcal{C}
. Then, like in Case
1
,
\lambda_{n}\theta_{B,n}
is a global minimum of
\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})
, so
\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})=0
. If
\lambda_{n}\theta_{B,n}
is outside
\mathcal{C}
, then

||\lambda_{n}\theta_{B,n}-\theta_{B,n}||_{2}=||\theta_{B,n}||_{2}-||\lambda_{n}\theta_{B,n}||_{2}\leq||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{q}}.

By looking at the two cases above, we see that
||\lambda_{n}\theta_{B,n}-\theta_{B,n}||_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{q}}
or
\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})=0
. But now, note that
\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})
is
K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}L_{x}^{2}
-smooth, and using Cauchy-Schwarz, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\lambda_{n}\theta_{B,n},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathcal{L}(\theta_{B,n},\mathcal{D}_{n})

\displaystyle\leq||\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta_{B,n},\mathcal{D}_{n})||_{2}||\lambda_{n}\theta_{B,n}-\theta_{B,n}||_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}L_{x}^{2}}{2}||\lambda_{n}\theta_{B,n}-\theta_{B,n}||_{2}^{2}.

Therefore, since
||\lambda_{n}\theta_{B,n}-\theta_{B,n}||_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{q}}
or
\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})=0
, and referring back to inequality (
D.1.4
), we have in all cases on
\Omega
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\theta_{T},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log(n/\delta)\sqrt{\log(n)\log(n/\zeta)}}{n^{1-q/2}\epsilon}

\displaystyle\quad+||\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta_{B,n},\mathcal{D}_{n})||_{2}\frac{1}{n^{q}}+\frac{1}{n^{2q}}.

(22)

We need to control
||\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta_{B,n},\mathcal{D}_{n})||_{2}
. For all
i\in[n]
, recall that
\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta^{*},z_{i})=(\Phi^{\prime}(x_{i}^{T}\theta^{*})-y_{i})x_{i}
. For
h\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, we have
|(\Phi^{\prime}(x_{i}^{T}\theta^{*})-y_{i})x_{i}^{T}h|\leq(K_{\Phi^{\prime}}+K_{y})L_{x}||h||_{2}
, so

\displaystyle(\Phi^{\prime}(x_{i}^{T}\theta^{*})-y_{i})x_{i}-\mathbb{E}[(\Phi^{\prime}(x_{i}^{T}\theta^{*})-y_{i})x_{i}]\in\mathcal{G}\left((K_{\Phi^{\prime}}+K_{y})^{2}L_{x}^{2}\right),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta^{*},z_{i})-\mathbb{E}[\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta^{*},z_{1})]=\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta^{*},\mathcal{D}_{n})\in\mathcal{G}\left(\frac{(K_{\Phi^{\prime}}+K_{y})^{2}L_{x}^{2}}{n}\right),

and by Lemma
15
, we have
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{3})\geq 1-\zeta
, where

\displaystyle\Omega_{3}=\left\{\left\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta^{*},\mathcal{D}_{n})\right\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{\frac{8(K_{\Phi^{\prime}}+K_{y})^{2}L_{x}^{2}\log(4^{p}/\zeta)}{n}}\right\}.

Let
\Omega^{\prime}=\Omega\cap\Omega_{3}
and
\mathbb{P}(\Omega^{\prime})\geq 1-2\zeta
. Now, using the
K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}L_{x}^{2}
-smoothness of
\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})
over
\mathbb{R}^{p}
, we have on
\Omega^{\prime}
that

\displaystyle||\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta_{B,n},\mathcal{D}_{n})||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq||\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta_{B,n},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta^{*},\mathcal{D}_{n})||_{2}+||\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta^{*},\mathcal{D}_{n})||_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim||\theta_{B,n}-\theta^{*}||_{2}+||\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta^{*},\mathcal{D}_{n})||_{2}\lesssim||\theta_{B,n}-\theta^{*}||_{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\zeta)}{n}}.

Hence, we need to control
||\theta_{B,n}-\theta^{*}||_{2}
. To do that, we want to use Lemma
24
, with the metric space given by
(\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2}),||\cdot||_{2})
, and we will check the conditions of that result. That is, we consider
\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})
with the induced
\ell_{2}
-norm metric from
\mathbb{R}^{p}
. We have
\theta^{*}=\mathop{\arg\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})}\mathcal{R}(\theta)
and
\theta_{B,n}\in\mathop{\arg\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})
. Also, because of the strong convexity of
\mathcal{R}
over a ball centered at
0
, as seen in Lemma
1
, and because
\theta^{*}
is the minimizer of
\mathcal{R}
over
\mathbb{R}^{p}
, as seen in Section
2.3.2
, we have
\mathcal{R}(\theta)-\mathcal{R}(\theta^{*})\gtrsim||\theta-\theta^{*}||_{2}^{2}
, for all
\theta
in a small enough neighborhood of
\theta^{*}
in the metric space
(\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2}),||\cdot||_{2})
. Now, observe that
\theta_{B,n}
is a maximum likelihood estimator (MLE) of
\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})
over
\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})
, since
\mathcal{L}
is the negative log-likelihood loss. Note that we satisfy the conditions of Lemma
23
, hence
\theta_{B,n}
converges in probability to
\theta^{*}
. Let
\mathcal{K}=\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2}+1)
. As in the proof of Theorem
4
, using a covering argument and inequality (
18
), we have for
Z_{\theta}=\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,z_{i})-\mathbb{E}[\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,z_{1})]\right\|_{2}=\left\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})-\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta)\right\|_{2}
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathop{\sup}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{K}}Z_{\theta}\geq t\right)\leq\left(\frac{64K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}L_{x}^{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2}+1)}{t}\right)^{p}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{32s_{n}^{2}}},\quad\forall\ t\leq 8K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}L_{x}^{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2}+1).

Since
||\theta^{*}||_{2}+1\asymp 1
, and by rescaling
t
, there are absolute constants
C_{4},C_{5}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathop{\sup}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{K}}Z_{\theta}\geq t\right)\leq\frac{1}{t^{p}}e^{-\frac{nt^{2}}{C_{4}}},\quad\forall\ t\leq C_{5}.

We want
t\leq C_{5}
and
\frac{1}{t^{p}}e^{-\frac{nt^{2}}{C_{4}}}\leq\frac{1}{n}
, or equivalently,
t^{2}+\frac{pC_{4}}{n}\log(t)\geq\frac{C_{4}}{n}\log(n)
. Take
t=\sqrt{\frac{C_{4}\log(n)}{n}}+\frac{\log(n)}{\sqrt{n}}
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle t^{2}+\frac{pC_{4}}{n}\log(t)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\log^{2}(n)}{n}+\frac{2\log(n)\sqrt{C_{4}\log(n)}}{n}+\frac{C_{4}\log(n)}{n}+\frac{pC_{4}}{2n}\log\left(\frac{\log(n)}{n}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{C_{4}\log(n)}{n},

for
n
large enough. This is because, for
n
large enough, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\log^{2}(n)}{n}\geq\frac{C_{4}}{2n}\log\left(\frac{n}{\log(n)}\right).

Note also that, for
n
large enough, we have
t\leq C_{5}
. Hence, there is an absolute constant
C_{6}>0
such that for any
n\geq C_{6}
, we have
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{4})\geq 1-\frac{1}{n}
, with

\displaystyle\Omega_{4}=\left\{\left\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})-\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta)\right\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{\frac{C_{4}\log(n)}{n}}+\frac{\log(n)}{\sqrt{n}},\quad\forall\theta\in\mathcal{K}\right\}.

Now take
u\leq 1
and let
\mathbb{U}_{n}(\theta)=\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathcal{R}(\theta)
. We have by the Mean Value Theorem that

\displaystyle\mathop{\sup}_{\begin{subarray}{c}||\theta-\theta^{*}||_{2}\leq u\\
\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})\end{subarray}}\left|\mathbb{U}_{n}(\theta)-\mathbb{U}_{n}(\theta^{*})\right|\leq\mathop{\sup}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{K}}\left\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})-\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta)\right\|_{2}u,

since the supremum only increases if we take it over
\mathcal{K}=\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2}+1)
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathop{\sup}_{\begin{subarray}{c}||\theta-\theta^{*}||_{2}\leq u\\
\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})\end{subarray}}\left|\mathbb{U}_{n}(\theta)-\mathbb{U}_{n}(\theta^{*})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\mathop{\sup}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{K}}\left\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})-\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta)\right\|_{2}u\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathop{\sup}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{K}}\left\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})-\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta)\right\|_{2}u\mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_{4}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\mathop{\sup}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{K}}\left\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})-\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta)\right\|_{2}u\mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_{4}^{c}}\right],

implying that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathop{\sup}_{\begin{subarray}{c}||\theta-\theta^{*}||_{2}\leq u\\
\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})\end{subarray}}\left|\mathbb{U}_{n}(\theta)-\mathbb{U}_{n}(\theta^{*})\right|\right]\lesssim\frac{\log(n)u}{\sqrt{n}}\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{4})+u\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{4}^{c})\leq\frac{\log(n)u}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{u}{n}\lesssim\frac{\log(n)u}{\sqrt{n}},

for all
0<u\leq 1
and
n\geq C_{6}
, since
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{R}
are
(K_{\Phi^{\prime}}+K_{y})L_{x}
-Lipschitz over
\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
(K_{\Phi^{\prime}}+K_{y})L_{x}\asymp 1
, as seen in Theorem
4
. Take
\phi_{n}(u)=\log(n)u
and
r_{n}=\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\log(n)}
. Note that
u\mapsto\frac{\phi_{n}(u)}{u}=\log(n)
is non-increasing and
r_{n}^{2}\phi_{n}\left(\frac{1}{r_{n}}\right)=r_{n}\log(n)=\sqrt{n}
. Hence, all the conditions of Lemma
24
are satisfied with
\alpha=1<2
, so for
\zeta\in(0,1/3)
, there are
T_{\zeta},N_{\zeta}>0
, such that
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{5})\geq 1-\zeta
, for all
n\geq\max\left\{C_{6},N_{\zeta}\right\}
, where

\displaystyle\Omega_{5}=\left\{||\theta_{B,n}-\theta^{*}||_{2}\leq\frac{T_{\zeta}\log(n)}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}.

Now we absorb
C^{\prime}_{1}
into
C_{6}
, i.e., relabel
\max\{C^{\prime}_{1},C_{6}\}
by
C^{\prime}_{1}
. Working on
\Omega^{\prime\prime}=\Omega^{\prime}\cap\Omega_{5}
, with
\mathbb{P}(\Omega^{\prime\prime})\geq 1-3\zeta
, we have for
n>\max\left\{C_{2}\log^{5}(2/\zeta),N_{\zeta},C^{\prime}_{1}\right\}
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\theta_{T},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log(n/\delta)\sqrt{\log(n)\log(n/\zeta)}}{n^{1-q/2}\epsilon}+\frac{T_{\zeta}\log(n)}{n^{q+\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\sqrt{\log(4/\zeta)}}{n^{q+\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{1}{n^{2q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(n/\delta)T_{\zeta}\sqrt{\log(n)\log(n/\zeta)}\left(\frac{1}{{n^{1-q/2}\epsilon}}+\frac{1}{n^{q+\frac{1}{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\log(n/\delta)T_{\zeta}\sqrt{\log(n)\log(n/\zeta)}}{n^{2q}},

by plugging back into inequality (
D.1.4
). Now, for
q=\frac{2}{5}
, since
0<\epsilon\lesssim 1
, we obtain the desired result.

Finally, using the assumption that
\epsilon\leq 0.9
, we have
\epsilon<2\sqrt{2T\log(2/\delta)}
and
\delta<2T
, where
T\asymp n^{q}\log(n)
, which are needed in Lemma
2
to ensure that the output of Algorithm
3
is
(\epsilon,\delta)
-DP.

Remark 19
.

We proved Theorem
5
by deriving a more general statement with
q<\frac{1}{2}
: based on this approach, the best choice is
q=\frac{2}{5}
. Indeed, examining the RHS of inequality (
19
), we can consider the lines
1-\frac{q}{2},q+\frac{1}{2}
and
2q
. In order to obtain a rate better than
\frac{1}{n^{2/3}}
up to logarithmic factors, we need
q>\frac{1}{3}
. Hence, to optimize the RHS of inequality (
19
) over
\frac{1}{3}<q<\frac{1}{2}
, we see that the best
q
is at the intersection of
1-\frac{q}{2}
and
2q
, namely
q=\frac{2}{5}
.

D.1.5
Proof of Theorem
6

The conditions in the theorem hypothesis are part of the ones in Theorem
5
. Hence, by Theorem
5
, we have with probability at least
1-3\zeta
, for
n>\max\left\{C_{2}\log^{5}(2/\zeta),N_{\zeta},C^{\prime}_{1}\right\}
, that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\theta_{T},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})\lesssim\frac{T_{\zeta}\log(n/\delta)\sqrt{\log(n)\log(n/\zeta)}}{n^{4/5}\epsilon}.

Let
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
be the event with probability at least
1-3\zeta
and
\mathbb{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times n}
be the matrix with
x_{i}
as the
i^{th}
row, for
i\in[n]
. Let
v\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
be such that
||v||_{2}=1
. Then

\displaystyle v^{T}\frac{\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}}{n}v

\displaystyle=v^{T}\Sigma v-v^{T}\left(\Sigma-\frac{\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}}{n}\right)v\geq\lambda_{\min}\left(\Sigma\right)-||v||_{2}^{2}\left\|\frac{\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}}{n}-\ \Sigma\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{\min}\left(\Sigma\right)-\left\|\frac{\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}}{n}-\ \Sigma\right\|_{2}.

Recall also that, in the context of the GLM defined in Section
2.3.2
,
\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)
and
\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma)
are positive absolute constants. Let
C_{4}=\frac{8L_{x}^{2}(\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma)+\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)/3)}{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)^{2}}
. Since
\{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are i.i.d.,
\mathbb{E}[x_{1}]=0
,
||\Sigma||_{2}=\lambda_{\max}\left(\Sigma\right)
, and
||x_{1}||_{2}\leq\sqrt{L_{x}^{2}}
, by Lemma
21
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}}{n}-\ \Sigma\right\|_{2}>\frac{\lambda_{\min}\left(\Sigma\right)}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}x_{i}^{T}-\ \Sigma\right\|_{2}>\frac{\lambda_{\min}\left(\Sigma\right)}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2pe^{\frac{-n\lambda_{\min}\left(\Sigma\right)^{2}}{8L_{x}^{2}\left(\lambda_{\max}\left(\Sigma\right)+\lambda_{\min}\left(\Sigma\right)/3\right)}}\leq 2pe^{-\frac{n}{C_{4}}}\leq\zeta,

since
n>C_{4}\log(2p/\zeta)
. Therefore, on
\Omega_{6}=\left\{\left\|\frac{\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}}{n}-\ \Sigma\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{\lambda_{\min}\left(\Sigma\right)}{2}\right\}
, we have for
n>C_{4}\log(2p/\zeta)
that
\lambda_{\min}\left(\frac{\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}}{n}\right)\geq\frac{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)}{2}
. Recall now from Theorem
4
that
\nabla^{2}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Phi^{\prime\prime}(x_{i}^{T}\theta)x_{i}x_{i}^{T}
. Using the properties of
\Phi^{\prime\prime}
outlined in Section
2.3.2
, we have
\Phi^{\prime\prime}(x^{T}\theta)\geq\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2})
, for all
\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})
. Hence, on
\Omega_{6}
, we see that for all
\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})
and
n>C_{4}\log(2p/\zeta)
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Phi^{\prime\prime}(x_{i}^{T}\theta)x_{i}x_{i}^{T}\succeq\frac{\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2})\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}}{n}\succeq\frac{\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2})\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)}{2}I_{p}.

Thus, on
\Omega_{6}
, the function
\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})
is
\frac{\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2})\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)}{2}
-strongly convex over
\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})
, for
n>C_{4}\log(2p/\zeta)
. Note that
\frac{\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2})\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)}{2}\asymp 1
. Let us now work on
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}=\Omega^{\prime\prime}\cap\Omega_{6}
, so that
\mathbb{P}(\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime})\geq 1-4\zeta
. Take
n>\max\left\{C_{2}\log^{5}(2/\zeta),C_{4}\log(2p/\zeta),N_{\zeta},C^{\prime}_{1}\right\}
. We had, using the notation from Theorem
5
, i.e.,
\theta_{B,n}\in\mathop{\arg\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})
, that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\theta_{T},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathcal{L}(\theta_{B,n},\mathcal{D}_{n})\lesssim\frac{T_{\zeta}\log(n/\delta)\sqrt{\log(n)\log(n/\zeta)}}{n^{4/5}\epsilon}.

Because of the strong convexity of
\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})
over
\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})
, and because
\theta_{B,n}
is a minimizer, we obtain

||\theta_{T}-\theta_{B,n}||_{2}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{L}(\theta_{T},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathcal{L}(\theta_{B,n},\mathcal{D}_{n}).

Recall now from the proof of Theorem
5
that
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
is an intersection of three events, each with probability at least
1-\zeta
, and on one of those we had
||\theta_{B,n}-\theta^{*}||_{2}\leq\frac{T_{\zeta}\log(n)}{\sqrt{n}}
. So, putting all this together, we obtain

\displaystyle||\theta_{T}-\theta^{*}||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq||\theta_{T}-\theta_{B,n}||_{2}+||\theta_{B,n}-\theta^{*}||_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{T_{\zeta}\log(n)}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{T_{\zeta}^{1/2}\log^{1/2}(n/\delta)\log^{1/4}(n)\log^{1/4}(n/\zeta)}{n^{2/5}\epsilon^{1/2}},

as required.

D.1.6
Proof of Theorem
7

Let
\zeta\in(0,1)
be arbitrary. To start off, by Theorem
4
with
q=\frac{1}{4}
, there exist positive absolute constants
C^{\prime}_{1}
and
C_{1}
such that for
C_{2}=\frac{\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2})\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)(||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D)}{4}>0
,
n>\max\left\{\left(\frac{\sqrt{C_{1}\log(2/\zeta)}+1}{C_{2}}\right)^{4},C^{\prime}_{1}\right\}
,
r\in\left(\frac{C_{2}}{2},C_{2}\right]
, and
T=\log_{1/c}(n)
, Algorithm
3
returns
\theta_{T}
such that with probability at least
1-\zeta
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\theta_{T},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})\lesssim\frac{1}{n}+\frac{\eta\log\left(\log_{1/c}(n)/\delta\right)\sqrt{\log_{1/c}(n)\log\left(\log_{1/c}(n)/\zeta\right)}}{(1-c)n\epsilon}.

(23)

This is because if
n>\left(\frac{\sqrt{C_{1}\log(2/\zeta)}+1}{C_{2}}\right)^{4}
, we have

\frac{\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2})\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)}{4}(||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D)n^{1/4}>\sqrt{C_{1}\log(2/\zeta)}+1\geq\frac{\sqrt{C_{1}\log(2/\zeta)}}{n^{1/4}}+1.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2})\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)}{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D)-\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}\log(2/\zeta)}{n}}-\frac{1}{n^{1/4}}

\displaystyle\quad>\frac{\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2})\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)}{4}(||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D)=C_{2},

and since
r\in\left(\frac{C_{2}}{2},C_{2}\right]
, we have

\displaystyle 0<r\leq\frac{\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2})\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)}{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D)-\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}\log(2/\zeta)}{n}}-\frac{1}{n^{1/4}}.

Moreover,
r>0
, since
\theta^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}\setminus\mathcal{C}
, so
||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D>0
. Thus, we can use Theorem
4
to conclude that inequality (
23
) holds with probability at least
1-\zeta
. Also,
\eta=\min\left\{1,\frac{\alpha_{\mathcal{C}}r}{4K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}L_{x}^{2}}\right\}
and
c=\max\left\{\frac{1}{2},1-\frac{\alpha_{\mathcal{C}}r}{8K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}L_{x}^{2}}\right\}
. Now, notice that

1\asymp\frac{\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2})\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)(||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D)}{8}<r\leq\frac{\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2})\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)(||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D)}{4}\asymp 1.

Thus,
r=\Theta(1)
. Since
\alpha_{\mathcal{C}}\asymp 1
as well, we have
\eta,c\asymp 1
. Hence, since
0<\epsilon\lesssim 1
, with probability at least
1-\zeta
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\theta_{T},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n}+\frac{\log\left(\log(n)/\delta\right)\sqrt{\log(n)\log\left(\log(n)/\zeta\right)}}{n\epsilon}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log\left(\log(n)/\delta\right)\sqrt{\log(n)\log\left(\log(n)/\zeta\right)}}{n\epsilon}.

Let
\Omega_{\zeta}
denote the event where the preceding bound holds. Taking
\zeta=\frac{1}{n}
, we see that for
n>\max\left\{\left(\frac{\sqrt{C_{1}\log(2n)}+1}{C_{2}}\right)^{4},C^{\prime}_{1}\right\}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{L}(\theta_{T},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathcal{L}(\theta_{T},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_{\zeta}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathcal{L}(\theta_{T},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\Omega^{c}_{\zeta}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log\left(\log(n)/\delta\right)\sqrt{\log(n)\log\left(n\log(n)\right)}}{n\epsilon}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{L}(\theta_{T},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})\right]}{n}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log\left(\log(n)/\delta\right)\sqrt{\log(n)\log\left(n\log(n)\right)}}{n\epsilon}+\frac{L_{2}||\mathcal{C}||_{2}}{n}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log\left(\log(n)/\delta\right)\sqrt{\log(n)\log\left(n\log(n)\right)}}{n\epsilon},

as required, where we used the
L_{2}
-Lipschitz property of the loss, together with the fact that
||\mathcal{C}||_{2}\lesssim||\theta^{*}||_{2}\asymp 1
.

Note that
\epsilon<2\sqrt{2T\log(2/\delta)}
and
\delta<2T
, since
T\asymp\log(n)
. Hence, by Lemma
2
,
\theta_{T}
is
(\epsilon,\delta)
-DP.

D.1.7
Proof of Theorem
8

In this context, we are working with i.i.d. samples
\mathcal{D}_{n}=\{z_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
and the squared error risk
\mathcal{R}(\theta)=\frac{1}{2}(\theta-\theta^{*})^{T}\Sigma(\theta-\theta^{*})+\frac{\sigma_{2}^{2}}{2}
. Fix
\theta\in\mathcal{C}
. Since we are using Algorithm
4
as gradient estimator, we have by Lemma
34
a
g
such that

\displaystyle\alpha(\widetilde{n},\widetilde{\zeta})\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\widetilde{\zeta})}{\widetilde{n}}},

\displaystyle\beta(\widetilde{n},\widetilde{\zeta})\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{2}^{2}\log(1/\widetilde{\zeta})}{\widetilde{n}}}.

Note that since
\theta^{*}\in\mathcal{C}
, we have
\theta_{*}=\theta^{*}
. At any
t\in\{1,\dots T\}
, with probability at least
1-\widetilde{\zeta}
, we have

\displaystyle||g(\theta_{t},\mathcal{D}_{n},\widetilde{\zeta})-\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta_{t})||_{2}\leq\alpha(\widetilde{n},\widetilde{\zeta})||\theta_{t}-\theta^{*}||_{2}+\beta(\widetilde{n},\widetilde{\zeta}).

Hence, by a union bound, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\exists t\ \mbox{s.t.}\ ||g(\theta_{t},\mathcal{D}_{n},\widetilde{\zeta})-\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta_{t})||_{2}>\alpha(\widetilde{n},\widetilde{\zeta})||\theta_{t}-\theta^{*}||_{2}+\beta(\widetilde{n},\widetilde{\zeta}))\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{\zeta}\leq\zeta,

implying that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\forall t,\ ||g(\theta_{t},\mathcal{D}_{n},\widetilde{\zeta})-\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta_{t})||_{2}\leq\alpha(\widetilde{n},\widetilde{\zeta})||\theta_{t}-\theta^{*}||_{2}+\beta(\widetilde{n},\widetilde{\zeta}))\geq 1-\zeta.

On the latter event, using the notation
\alpha=\alpha(\widetilde{n},\widetilde{\zeta}),\beta=\beta(\widetilde{n},\widetilde{\zeta})
and ignoring the dependency in
g
on the samples and
\widetilde{\zeta}
, the gradient error
e_{t}:=g(\theta_{t})-\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta_{t})
satisfies

\displaystyle||e_{t}||_{2}\leq\alpha||\theta_{t}-\theta^{*}||_{2}+\beta,

implying that

\displaystyle v_{t}^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta_{t})

\displaystyle\leq v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta_{t})+(v-v_{t})^{T}e_{t}\leq v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta_{t})+||v-v_{t}||_{2}||e_{t}||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta_{t})+||\mathcal{C}||_{2}(\alpha||\mathcal{C}||_{2}+\beta),\quad\forall v\in\mathcal{C}.

Let
\Gamma_{\mathcal{R}}
be the curvature constant of
\mathcal{R}
. We then have

\displaystyle v_{t}^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\min}\limits_{v\in\mathcal{C}}v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta_{t})+\frac{1}{2}\frac{2}{t+2}\Gamma_{\mathcal{R}}\frac{||\mathcal{C}||_{2}(\alpha||\mathcal{C}||_{2}+\beta)}{\Gamma_{\mathcal{R}}}(t+2)

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\min}\limits_{v\in\mathcal{C}}v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta_{t})+\frac{1}{2}\frac{2}{t+2}\Gamma_{\mathcal{R}}\frac{||\mathcal{C}||_{2}(\alpha||\mathcal{C}||_{2}+\beta)}{\Gamma_{\mathcal{R}}}(T+2).

Thus, on the event with probability at least
1-\zeta
, since
\mathcal{C}
is compact and convex, by Lemma
9
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\theta_{T})-\mathcal{R}(\theta^{*})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\Gamma_{\mathcal{R}}}{T+2}\left(1+\frac{||\mathcal{C}||_{2}(\alpha||\mathcal{C}||_{2}+\beta)}{\Gamma_{\mathcal{R}}}(T+2)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\Gamma_{\mathcal{R}}}{T+2}+2||\mathcal{C}||_{2}(\alpha||\mathcal{C}||_{2}+\beta).

Now note that
\mathcal{R}(\theta)
is a quadratic in
\theta
with second-order term
\frac{1}{2}\theta^{T}\Sigma\theta=\theta^{T}\Sigma^{1/2}\Sigma^{1/2}\theta
.
By Remark
2
in
[
52
]
, we have
\Gamma_{\mathcal{R}}\leq 4\mathop{\max}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\theta\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim 1
. Thus, since
||\mathcal{C}||_{2}\lesssim 1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\theta_{T})-\mathcal{R}(\theta^{*})\lesssim\frac{1}{T}+(1+\sigma_{2})\sqrt{\frac{T\log(T/\zeta)}{n}},

and since
T=n^{1/3}
, this implies

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\theta_{T})-\mathcal{R}(\theta^{*})\lesssim\frac{(1+\sigma_{2})\sqrt{\log(n/\zeta)}}{n^{1/3}}.

By
\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)
-strong convexity of
\mathcal{R}
, because
\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta^{*})=0
,
\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)\asymp 1
, and
\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)>0
we have

\displaystyle||\theta_{T}-\theta^{*}||_{2}\lesssim\frac{(1+\sigma_{2})^{1/2}\log^{1/4}(n/\zeta)}{n^{1/6}},

as required.

D.1.8
Proof of Theorem
9

Recall the notation
\theta_{*}=\mathop{\arg\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{R}(\theta)
. Following the same steps as in the proof of Theorem
8
, we have with probability at least
1-\zeta
at the
t^{\text{th}}
step of Algorithm
5
that

\displaystyle v_{t}^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta_{t})

\displaystyle\leq v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta_{t})+(v-v_{t})^{T}e_{t}\leq v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta_{t})+||v-v_{t}||_{2}||e_{t}||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta_{t})+||\mathcal{C}||_{2}(\alpha||\theta_{t}-\theta_{*}||_{2}+\beta)

\displaystyle\leq v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta_{t})+||\mathcal{C}||_{2}(\alpha||\mathcal{C}||_{2}+\beta),\ \quad\forall v\in\mathcal{C}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle v_{t}^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta_{t})\leq\mathop{\min}\limits_{v\in\mathcal{C}}v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta_{t})+||\mathcal{C}||_{2}(\alpha||\mathcal{C}||_{2}+\beta).

Now note that for
\theta\in\mathcal{C}
, we have

\displaystyle||\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta)||_{2}

\displaystyle=||\Sigma(\theta^{*}-\theta)||_{2}\geq\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)\left(||\theta^{*}||_{2}-||\theta||_{2}\right)\geq\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)\left(||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{C_{1}\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)}{n^{1/5}}\geq u\gtrsim\frac{1}{n^{1/5}}.

Thus, with probability at least
1-\zeta
, since
\mathcal{C}
is compact and
\frac{1}{D}
-strongly convex by Lemma
5
and
\mathcal{R}
is
\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma)
-smooth, we are in the context of Theorem
1
. For the choice of
\eta
in the theorem hypothesis, Theorem
1
then implies

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\theta_{t})-\mathcal{R}(\theta_{*})\leq\left(\mathcal{R}(\theta_{0})-\mathcal{R}(\theta_{*})\right)c^{t}+\frac{3\eta||\mathcal{C}||_{2}(\alpha||\mathcal{C}||_{2}+\beta)}{2(1-c)},

with
c=\max\left\{\frac{1}{2},1-\frac{\alpha_{\mathcal{C}}u}{8\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma)}\right\}
. Note that since
u\asymp\frac{1}{n^{1/5}}
,
\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma)\asymp 1
, and
D\asymp 1
, we have
\eta\asymp\frac{1}{n^{1/5}}
and
c\asymp 1-\frac{1}{n^{1/5}}
, so
\frac{1}{1-c}\asymp n^{1/5}
. Also,
\mathcal{R}(\theta_{0})-\mathcal{R}(\theta_{*}),||\mathcal{C}||_{2}\lesssim 1
. Thus, at iteration
T
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\theta_{T})-\mathcal{R}(\theta_{*})\lesssim c^{T}+(1+\sigma_{2})\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\widetilde{\zeta})}{\widetilde{n}}}.

Note that now
\log(1/c)\asymp\frac{1}{n^{1/5}}\log\left(\left(1-\frac{1}{n^{1/5}}\right)^{-n^{1/5}}\right)\asymp\frac{1}{n^{1/5}}
. Since
T=\log_{1/c}\left(n^{2/5}\right)\asymp n^{1/5}\log(n)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\theta_{T})-\mathcal{R}(\theta_{*})\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2/5}}+(1+\sigma_{2})\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)\log\left(n\log(n)/\zeta\right)}{n^{4/5}}}.

Now define
\mathcal{A}:[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, as
\mathcal{A}(\lambda)=\mathcal{R}(\lambda\theta^{*})
. Note that
\mathcal{A}(0)=\mathcal{R}(0)\geq\mathcal{R}(\theta_{*})\geq\mathcal{R}(\theta^{*})=\mathcal{A}(1)
. So, by the continuity of
\mathcal{A}
, the Intermediate Value Theorem implies that there exists
\lambda_{*}\in[0,1]
such that
\mathcal{A}(\lambda_{*})=\mathcal{R}(\lambda_{*}\theta^{*})=\mathcal{R}(\theta_{*})=\mathop{\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{R}(\theta)
. If
\lambda_{*}\theta^{*}
is in the interior of
\mathcal{C}
, then
\nabla\mathcal{R}(\lambda_{*}\theta^{*})=0
, so
\lambda_{*}\theta^{*}
is a global minimizer. This is a contradiction, since
\theta^{*}
is the unique global minimizer of
\mathcal{R}
and thus lies strictly outside
\mathcal{C}
. If
\lambda_{*}\theta^{*}
is at the boundary or outside
\mathcal{C}
, then
||\lambda_{*}\theta^{*}-\theta^{*}||_{2}\leq||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{1/5}}
. Hence, by the
\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma)
-smoothness of
\mathcal{R}
, using the fact that
\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta^{*})=0
and that
\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma)\asymp 1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\theta_{*})-\mathcal{R}(\theta^{*})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}(\lambda_{*}\theta^{*})-\mathcal{R}(\theta^{*})\lesssim||\lambda_{*}\theta_{*}-\theta_{*}||_{2}^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2/5}}.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\theta_{T})-\mathcal{R}(\theta^{*})\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2/5}}+(1+\sigma_{2})\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)\log\left(n\log(n)/\zeta\right)}{n^{4/5}}}+\frac{1}{n^{2/5}},

(24)

and by the
\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)
-strong convexity of
\mathcal{R}
over
\mathbb{R}^{p}
, together with
\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta^{*})=0
and
\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)\asymp 1
, we obtain

\displaystyle||\theta_{T}-\theta^{*}||_{2}\lesssim\frac{(1+\sigma_{2})^{1/2}\log^{1/4}(n)\log^{1/4}\left(n\log(n)/\zeta\right)}{n^{1/5}},

as required.

Remark 20
.

The choice of the exponent
\frac{1}{5}
in
||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{1/5}}
,
D\leq||\theta^{*}||_{2}-\frac{C_{1}}{n^{1/5}}
,
T=\log_{1/c}(n^{2/5})\asymp n^{1/5}\log(n)
, and
\frac{1}{n^{1/5}}\lesssim u\leq\frac{C_{1}\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)}{n^{1/5}}
is not arbitrary. Assume we started with
||\theta^{*}||_{2}-D\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{q}}
,
D\leq||\theta^{*}||_{2}-\frac{C_{1}}{n^{q}}
,
T=\log_{1/c}(n^{2q})\asymp n^{q}\log(n)
, and
\frac{1}{n^{q}}\lesssim u\leq\frac{C_{1}\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)}{n^{q}}
, for some
q>0
. Then inequality (
24
) becomes

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\theta_{T})-\mathcal{R}(\theta^{*})\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2q}}+(1+\sigma_{2})\frac{\sqrt{\log(n)\log\left(n\log(n)/\zeta\right)}}{n^{\frac{1-q}{2}}}+\frac{1}{n^{2q}}.

To minimize the RHS over
q>0
, we need to look at the intersection of the lines
\frac{1-q}{2}
and
2q
. This leads to the optimal value
q=\frac{1}{5}
.

D.1.9
Proof of Theorem
10

Here,
\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta)=\frac{1}{2}(\theta-\theta^{*})^{T}\Sigma(\theta-\theta^{*})+\frac{\sigma_{2}^{2}}{2}+\frac{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}||\theta||_{2}^{2}}{2}
. Since the global minimum of
\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}
is
\theta_{*}=(\Sigma+\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}I_{p})^{-1}\Sigma\theta^{*}
, minimizing
\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}
over
\mathbb{R}^{p}
is equivalent to minimizing over
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{B}_{2}\left(D\right)
, with
D\geq||(\Sigma+\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}I_{p})^{-1}\Sigma\theta^{*}||_{2}
. From Lemma
3
, we have a gradient estimator
g(\theta)
with

\displaystyle\alpha(\widetilde{n},\widetilde{\zeta})\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\widetilde{\zeta})}{\widetilde{n}}},

\displaystyle\beta(\widetilde{n},\widetilde{\zeta})\asymp\sqrt{\frac{(1+\sigma_{2}^{2})\log(1/\widetilde{\zeta})}{\widetilde{n}}},

since
\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)=0
. Thus, by a union bound, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\forall t,\ ||g(\theta_{t},\mathcal{D}_{n},\widetilde{\zeta})-\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{t})||_{2}\leq\alpha(\widetilde{n},\widetilde{\zeta})||\theta_{t}-\theta_{*}||_{2}+\beta(\widetilde{n},\widetilde{\zeta}))\geq 1-\zeta.

On the latter event, using the notation
\alpha=\alpha(\widetilde{n},\widetilde{\zeta})
and
\beta=\beta(\widetilde{n},\widetilde{\zeta})
and ignoring the dependency in
g
on the samples and
\widetilde{\zeta}
, we can bound the gradient
e_{t}:=g(\theta_{t})-\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{t})
as

\displaystyle||g(\theta_{t})-\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{t})||_{2}=||e_{t}||_{2}\leq\alpha||\theta_{t}-\theta_{*}||_{2}+\beta.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle v_{t}^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{t})

\displaystyle\leq v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{t})+(v-v_{t})^{T}e_{t}\leq v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{t})+||v-v_{t}||_{2}||e_{t}||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{t})+||\mathcal{C}||_{2}(2\alpha D+\beta),\ \quad\forall v\in\mathcal{C}.

Let
\Gamma_{\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}}
be the curvature constant of
\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}
. We then have

\displaystyle v_{t}^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\min}\limits_{v\in\mathcal{C}}v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{t})+\frac{1}{2}\frac{2}{t+2}\Gamma_{\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}}\frac{||\mathcal{C}||_{2}(2\alpha D+\beta)}{\Gamma_{\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}}}(t+2)

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\min}\limits_{v\in\mathcal{C}}v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{t})+\frac{1}{2}\frac{2}{t+2}\Gamma_{\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}}\frac{||\mathcal{C}||_{2}(2\alpha D+\beta)}{\Gamma_{\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}}}(T+2).

Thus, on the event with probability at least
1-\zeta
, since
\mathcal{C}
is compact and convex, by Lemma
9
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{T})-\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\Gamma_{\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}}}{T+2}\left(1+\frac{||\mathcal{C}||_{2}(2\alpha D+\beta)}{\Gamma_{\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}}}(T+2)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\Gamma_{\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}}}{T+2}+2||\mathcal{C}||_{2}(2\alpha D+\beta),

with
\theta_{*}=(\Sigma+\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}I_{p})^{-1}\Sigma\theta^{*}
. Now note that
\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta)
is a quadratic in
\theta
with the second-order term given by
\frac{1}{2}\theta^{T}(\Sigma+\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}I_{p})\theta=\frac{1}{2}\theta^{T}(\Sigma+\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}I_{p})^{1/2}(\Sigma+\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}I_{p})^{1/2}\theta
. By Remark
2
in
[
52
]
, we have
\Gamma_{\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}}\leq 4\mathop{\max}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\left\|(\Sigma+\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}I_{p})^{1/2}\theta\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim 1
. Thus, since
\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow 0
and
2D=||\mathcal{C}||_{2},||\theta^{*}||_{2}\lesssim 1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{T})-\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*})\lesssim\frac{1}{T}+(1+\sigma_{2})\sqrt{\frac{T\log(T/\zeta)}{n}},

and since
T=n^{1/3}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{T})-\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*})\lesssim\frac{(1+\sigma_{2})\sqrt{\log(n/\zeta)}}{n^{1/3}}.

Using the
\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}
-strong convexity of
\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}
and the fact that
\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*})=0
, we obtain

\displaystyle||\theta_{T}-\theta_{*}||_{2}\lesssim\frac{(1+\sigma_{2})^{1/2}\log^{1/4}(n/\zeta)}{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}^{1/2}n^{1/6}}.

Now, note that since
\theta_{*}=(\Sigma+\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}I_{p})^{-1}\Sigma\theta^{*}
, we obtain

\displaystyle||\theta_{*}-\theta^{*}||_{2}^{2}=||((S+\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}I_{p})^{-1}S-I_{p})P^{T}\theta^{*}||_{2}^{2}\lesssim m\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}+\left\|[P^{T}\theta^{*}]_{[(m+1):p]}\right\|_{2}^{2},

implying that

\displaystyle||\theta_{T}-\theta^{*}||_{2}\lesssim\frac{(1+\sigma_{2})^{1/2}\log^{1/4}(n/\zeta)}{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}^{1/2}n^{1/6}}+\sqrt{m}\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}+\left\|[P^{T}\theta^{*}]_{[(m+1):p]}\right\|_{2}.

(25)

For
\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}\gtrsim\frac{1}{n^{1/9}}
, obtain the desired bound.

Remark 21
.

Note that our choice for the value of
\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}
in the bound on
||\theta_{T}-\theta^{*}||_{2}
is based on the fact that the RHS quantity in inequality (
25
) is a decreasing function of
\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}
, for
\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}
small enough, i.e., for
n
large enough.

Additionally, we can comment on the choice of
\mathcal{C}
. We take
\mathcal{C}
to be an
\ell_{2}
-ball with radius
D\geq||(\Sigma+\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}I_{p})^{-1}\Sigma\theta^{*}||_{2}
. In Theorem
10
, we take
\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}\gtrsim\frac{1}{n^{1/9}}
. In practice, if we pick
\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}=\frac{1}{n^{1/9}}
and
D
large enough, we can carry out the optimization from Theorem
10
.

D.1.10
Proof of Theorem
11

Note that since
\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}\leq\frac{c_{\mathcal{K}}}{2}<c_{\mathcal{K}}
, we have
\mathcal{K}\subseteq\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta_{*}||_{2})
. Recall that
\tau_{u}=\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma)+\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}
,
\tau_{l}=\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}
, and
\theta_{*}=(\Sigma+\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}I_{p})^{-1}\Sigma\theta^{*}
. Following the same steps as in the proof of Theorem
10
, with probability at least
1-\zeta
, we have at the
t^{\text{th}}
step of Algorithm
5
that

\displaystyle v_{t}^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{t})

\displaystyle\leq v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{t})+(v-v_{t})^{T}e_{t}\leq v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{t})+||v-v_{t}||_{2}||e_{t}||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{t})+||\mathcal{K}||_{2}(2\alpha||\theta^{*}||_{2}+\beta),\quad\forall v\in\mathcal{K}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle v_{t}^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{t})\leq\mathop{\min}\limits_{v\in\mathcal{K}}v^{T}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{t})+||\mathcal{K}||_{2}(2\alpha||\theta^{*}||_{2}+\beta).

Now note that for
\theta\in\mathcal{K}
, and by the
\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}
-strong convexity of
\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle||\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta)||_{2}

\displaystyle=||\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta)-\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*})||_{2}\geq\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}||\theta-\theta_{*}||_{2}\geq\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}(||\theta_{*}||_{2}-||\theta||_{2})

\displaystyle\geq\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}\left(||(\Sigma+\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}I_{p})^{-1}\Sigma\theta^{*}||_{2}-||(\Sigma+c_{\mathcal{K}}I_{p})^{-1}\Sigma\theta^{*}||_{2}\right),

(26)

since
||\theta||_{2}\leq||(\Sigma+c_{\mathcal{K}}I_{p})^{-1}\Sigma\theta^{*}||_{2}
for all
\theta\in\mathcal{K}
. Also, the RHS of inequality (
D.1.10
) is positive, since
\mathcal{K}\subsetneq\mathcal{C}
. Hence, using the decomposition of
\Sigma
, we obtain

\displaystyle||\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta)||_{2}\geq\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}\left(||(S+\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}I_{p})^{-1}SP^{T}\theta^{*}||_{2}-||(S+c_{\mathcal{K}}I_{p})^{-1}SP^{T}\theta^{*}||_{2}\right).

Define
f:(0,c_{\mathcal{K}}]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that
f(z)=||(S+zI_{p})^{-1}SP^{T}\theta^{*}||_{2}
. We have, for
[P^{T}\theta^{*}]_{j}
being the
j^{\text{th}}
entry in
P^{T}\theta^{*}
, that

\displaystyle f(z)=\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{S_{jj}^{2}[P^{T}\theta^{*}]_{j}^{2}}{(S_{jj}+z)^{2}}},

\displaystyle|f^{\prime}(z)|=\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{S_{jj}^{2}[P^{T}\theta^{*}]_{j}^{2}}{(S_{jj}+z)^{3}}}{\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{S_{jj}^{2}[P^{T}\theta^{*}]_{j}^{2}}{(S_{jj}+z)^{2}}}}\geq\frac{\frac{S_{mm}^{2}}{(S_{mm}+c_{\mathcal{K}})^{3}}\left\|[P^{T}\theta^{*}]_{[1:m]}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{\left\|[P^{T}\theta^{*}]_{[1:m]}\right\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad=\frac{S_{mm}^{2}\left\|[P^{T}\theta^{*}]_{[1:m]}\right\|_{2}}{(S_{mm}+c_{\mathcal{K}})^{3}},\quad\forall z\in(0,c_{\mathcal{K}}].

(27)

Hence, by the Mean Value Theorem, using the lower bound on
|f^{\prime}|
and the fact that
\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}\leq\frac{c_{\mathcal{K}}}{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle||\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta)||_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}\frac{S_{mm}^{2}\left\|[P^{T}\theta^{*}]_{[1:m]}\right\|_{2}}{(S_{mm}+c_{\mathcal{K}})^{3}}(c_{\mathcal{K}}-\gamma_{\mathcal{C}})

\displaystyle\geq\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}\frac{S_{mm}^{2}\left\|[P^{T}\theta^{*}]_{[1:m]}\right\|_{2}c_{\mathcal{K}}}{2(S_{mm}+c_{\mathcal{K}})^{3}}\geq u,\quad\forall\theta\in\mathcal{K}.

Thus, with probability at least
1-\zeta
, since
\mathcal{K}
is compact and
\alpha_{\mathcal{K}}
-strongly convex by Lemma
5
, and
\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}
is
(\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma)+\gamma_{\mathcal{C}})
-smooth, we are in the context of Theorem
1
. Let
\theta_{*,\mathcal{K}}
be the minimum of
\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}
in
\mathcal{K}
. Thus, for the choice of
\eta
in the theorem hypothesis, Theorem
1
implies that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{t})-\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*,\mathcal{K}})\leq\left(\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{0})-\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*,\mathcal{K}})\right)c^{t}+\frac{3\eta||\mathcal{K}||_{2}(2\alpha||\theta^{*}||_{2}+\beta)}{2(1-c)},

with
c=\max\left\{\frac{1}{2},1-\frac{\alpha_{\mathcal{K}}u}{8(\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma)+\gamma_{\mathcal{C}})}\right\}
. Note that since
\left\|\left(\Sigma+C_{1}c_{\mathcal{K}}I_{p}\right)^{-1}\Sigma\theta^{*}\right\|_{2}\leq K\leq\left\|\left(\Sigma+c_{\mathcal{K}}I_{p}\right)^{-1}\Sigma\theta^{*}\right\|_{2}
,
\alpha_{\mathcal{K}}=\frac{1}{K}
, and
\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}c_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim u\leq\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}\frac{S_{mm}^{2}\left\|[P^{T}\theta^{*}]_{[1:m]}\right\|_{2}c_{\mathcal{K}}}{2(S_{mm}+c_{\mathcal{K}})^{3}}
, we have
\alpha_{\mathcal{K}}u\asymp\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}c_{\mathcal{K}}
. Thus,
\eta\asymp\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}c_{\mathcal{K}}
and
\frac{1}{1-c}\asymp\frac{1}{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}c_{\mathcal{K}}}
. By smoothness, because
\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*})=0
and
\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma)+\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}\lesssim 1
, we then obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{0})-\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*,\mathcal{K}})

\displaystyle\lesssim||\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*,\mathcal{K}})-\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*})||_{2}||\theta_{0}-\theta_{*,\mathcal{K}}||_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+(\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma)+\gamma_{\mathcal{C}})||\theta_{0}-\theta_{*,\mathcal{K}}||_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(||\theta_{*,\mathcal{K}}||_{2}+||\theta_{*}||_{2})||\mathcal{K}||_{2}+||\mathcal{K}||_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim||\theta^{*}||_{2}||\mathcal{K}||_{2}+||\mathcal{K}||_{2}^{2}\lesssim 1.

Thus, at iteration
T
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{T})-\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*,\mathcal{K}})\lesssim c^{T}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\widetilde{\zeta})}{\widetilde{n}}}+\sqrt{\frac{(1+\sigma_{2})^{2}\log(1/\widetilde{\zeta})}{\widetilde{n}}}.

Note that
\log(1/c)\asymp\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}c_{\mathcal{K}}\log\left((1-\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}c_{\mathcal{K}})^{1/\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}c_{\mathcal{K}}}\right)\asymp\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}c_{\mathcal{K}}
. Hence, since
T=\log_{1/c}\left(n\right)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{T})-\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*,\mathcal{K}})\lesssim\frac{1}{n}+(1+\sigma_{2})\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)\log\left(\log(n)/\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}\zeta\right)}{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}c_{\mathcal{K}}n}}.

Now define
\mathcal{A}:[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by
\mathcal{A}(\lambda)=\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\lambda\theta_{*})
. Note that

\mathcal{A}(0)=\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(0)\geq\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*,\mathcal{K}})\geq\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*})=\mathcal{A}(1).

Hence, by the continuity of
\mathcal{A}
, the Intermediate Value Theorem implies that there exists
\lambda_{*}\in[0,1]
such that
\mathcal{A}(\lambda_{*})=\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\lambda_{*}\theta_{*})=\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*,\mathcal{K}})
. If
\lambda_{*}\theta_{*}
is in the interior of
\mathcal{K}
, then
\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\lambda_{*}\theta_{*})=0
, so
\lambda_{*}\theta_{*}
is a global minimizer. This is a contradiction, since
\theta_{*}
is the unique global minimizer of
\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}
and this lies strictly outside
\mathcal{K}
. If
\lambda_{*}\theta_{*}
is at the boundary or outside
\mathcal{K}
, then

||\lambda_{*}\theta_{*}-\theta_{*}||_{2}\leq||\theta_{*}||_{2}-K\leq\left\|\left(\Sigma+\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}I_{p}\right)^{-1}\Sigma\theta^{*}\right\|_{2}-\left\|\left(\Sigma+C_{1}c_{\mathcal{K}}I_{p}\right)^{-1}\Sigma\theta^{*}\right\|_{2}.

By inequality (
D.1.10
), the Mean Value Theorem, and the fact that
C_{1}c_{\mathcal{K}}>c_{\mathcal{K}}>\frac{c_{\mathcal{K}}}{2}\geq\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}\geq\frac{c_{\mathcal{K}}}{4}
, there exists some
z_{*}\in\left[\gamma_{\mathcal{C}},C_{1}c_{\mathcal{K}}\right]
such that

\displaystyle||\lambda_{*}\theta_{*}-\theta_{*}||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{S_{jj}^{2}[P^{T}\theta^{*}]_{j}^{2}}{(S_{jj}+z_{*})^{3}}}{\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{S_{jj}^{2}[P^{T}\theta^{*}]_{j}^{2}}{(S_{jj}+z_{*})^{2}}}}(C_{1}c_{\mathcal{K}}-\gamma_{\mathcal{C}})\leq\frac{\frac{\left\|[P^{T}\theta^{*}]_{[1:m]}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{S_{mm}}}{\sqrt{\frac{S^{2}_{mm}\left\|[P^{T}\theta^{*}]_{[1:m]}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{(S_{11}+C_{1}c_{\mathcal{K}})^{2}}}}\left(C_{1}-\frac{1}{4}\right)c_{\mathcal{K}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(S_{11}+C_{1}c_{\mathcal{K}})\left\|[P^{T}\theta^{*}]_{[1:m]}\right\|_{2}}{S^{2}_{mm}}\left(C_{1}-\frac{1}{4}\right)c_{\mathcal{K}}\asymp c_{\mathcal{K}}.

Additionally, by the
(\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma)+\gamma_{\mathcal{C}})
-smoothness of
\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}
, and using the facts that
\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*})=0
and
\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma)+\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}\lesssim 1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*,\mathcal{K}})-\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\lambda_{*}\theta_{*})-\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*})\lesssim||\lambda_{*}\theta_{*}-\theta_{*}||_{2}^{2}\lesssim c_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{T})-\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n}+(1+\sigma_{2})\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)\log\left(\log(n)/\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}\zeta\right)}{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}c_{\mathcal{K}}n}}+c_{\mathcal{K}}^{2}.

Using the
\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}
-strong convexity of
\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}
and the fact that
\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}}(\theta_{*})=0
, we obtain

\displaystyle||\theta_{T}-\theta_{*}||_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}^{1/2}n^{1/2}}+(1+\sigma_{2})^{1/2}\frac{\log^{1/4}(n)\log^{1/4}\left(\log(n)/\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}\zeta\right)}{c_{\mathcal{K}}^{1/4}\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}^{3/4}n^{1/4}}+\frac{c_{\mathcal{K}}}{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}^{1/2}},

and since
||\theta_{*}-\theta^{*}||_{2}^{2}\lesssim m\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}+\left\|[P^{T}\theta^{*}]_{[(m+1):p]}\right\|_{2}^{2}
, we then have

\displaystyle||\theta_{T}-\theta^{*}||_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}^{1/2}n^{1/2}}+(1+\sigma_{2})^{1/2}\frac{\log^{1/4}(n)\log^{1/4}\left(\log(n)/\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}\zeta\right)}{c_{\mathcal{K}}^{1/4}\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}^{3/4}n^{1/4}}+\frac{c_{\mathcal{K}}}{\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}^{1/2}}+\sqrt{m}\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|[P^{T}\theta^{*}]_{[(m+1):p]}\right\|_{2}.

(28)

Then, for
\gamma_{\mathcal{C}}\geq\frac{c_{\mathcal{K}}}{4}
, we obtain

\displaystyle||\theta_{T}-\theta^{*}||_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\sigma_{2})^{1/2}\frac{\log^{1/4}(n)\log^{1/4}\left(n/\zeta\right)}{c_{\mathcal{K}}^{1/4}n^{1/4}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|[P^{T}\theta^{*}]_{[(m+1):p]}\right\|_{2}+\left\|[P^{T}\theta^{*}]_{[(m+1):p]}\right\|_{2}^{1/2},

as required.

D.2
Proofs of the Auxiliary Results from Section
3

Here, we present the proofs of the auxiliary results from Section
3
.

D.2.1
Proof of Lemma
2

Observe that for all
\theta
,
z_{1},\dots,z_{n}
,
z^{\prime}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,z_{j})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,z_{j})+\frac{1}{n}\nabla\mathcal{L}(z_{1},\theta)-\frac{1}{n}\nabla\mathcal{L}(z^{\prime}_{1},\theta)\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{2L_{2}}{n},

since the loss is
L_{2}
-Lipschitz. Hence, the sensitivity is bounded above by
\frac{2L_{2}}{n}
, and by Lemma
11
, since
\epsilon<2\sqrt{2T\log(2/\delta)}
and
\delta<2T
, each step of Algorithm
3
is
\left(\frac{\epsilon}{2\sqrt{2T\log(2/\delta)}},\frac{\delta}{2T}\right)
-DP. Hence, using Lemma
13
, i.e., the advanced composition result, we obtain that
\theta_{T}
is
\left(\frac{\epsilon}{2}+\frac{\sqrt{T}\epsilon}{2\sqrt{2\log(2/\delta)}}(e^{\epsilon/2\sqrt{2T\log(2/\delta)}}-1),\delta\right)
-DP. Finally, for
\epsilon\leq 0.9
, using Corollary
1
, we conclude that
\theta_{T}
is
(\epsilon,\delta)
-DP.

D.2.2
Proof of Proposition
1

Let
\mathbb{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times p}
be the matrix with
i^{\text{th}}
row being
x_{i}
, for all
i\in[n]
. Let
\mathbb{Y}=(y_{1},\dots,y_{n})^{T}
and
\mathbb{W}^{(p)}=\left(w_{1}^{(p)},\dots,w_{p}^{(p)}\right)^{T}
. Let
v\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
be such that
||v||_{2}=1
. Then we have

\displaystyle v^{T}\frac{\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}}{n}v

\displaystyle=v^{T}\Sigma v+v^{T}\left(\frac{\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}}{n}-\Sigma\right)v\leq\lambda_{\max}\left(\Sigma\right)+\left\|\frac{\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}}{n}-\ \Sigma\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}+\left\|\frac{\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}}{n}-\ \Sigma\right\|_{2},

and

\displaystyle v^{T}\frac{\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}}{n}v

\displaystyle=v^{T}\Sigma v-v^{T}\left(\Sigma-\frac{\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}}{n}\right)v\geq\lambda_{\min}\left(\Sigma\right)-\left\|\frac{\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}}{n}-\ \Sigma\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq C_{1}-\left\|\frac{\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}}{n}-\ \Sigma\right\|_{2}.

Note that since
||x_{1}||_{\infty}\leq 1
, we have
||x_{1}||_{2}\leq\sqrt{p}
.
By Lemma
21
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}}{n}-\ \Sigma\right\|_{2}>\frac{C_{1}}{2}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}x_{i}^{T}-\ \Sigma\right\|_{2}>\frac{C_{1}}{2}\right)\leq 2pe^{\frac{-nC_{1}^{2}}{8p\left(C_{2}+C_{1}/3\right)}}\rightarrow 0

as
p\rightarrow\infty
, since
n=\widetilde{\Omega}(p^{c_{1}})
and
c_{1}>1
. Hence, with probability at least
1-2pe^{\frac{-nC_{1}^{2}}{8p\left(C_{2}+C_{1}/3\right)}}
, we have
\left\|\frac{\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}}{n}-\ \Sigma\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{C_{1}}{2}
, so

\frac{C_{1}}{2}\leq\frac{\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}\right)}{n}\leq\frac{\lambda_{\max}\left(\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}\right)}{n}\leq\frac{2C_{2}+C_{1}}{2},

since
||v||_{2}=1
was arbitrary. Let
\Omega_{1}
be this event which occurs with probability at least
1-2pe^{\frac{-nC_{1}^{2}}{8p\left(C_{2}+C_{1}/3\right)}}
. Now recall that
\left\{w_{i}^{(p)}\right\}_{i=1}^{n}
are i.i.d. and
w_{i}^{(p)}\in\mathcal{G}\left(\sigma^{2}(p)\right)
, for all
i\in[n]
. Hence, by a union bound, Lemma
14
, and the fact that
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{W}^{(p)}\right]=0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\mathbb{W}^{(p)}\right\|_{\infty}>\frac{D(p)\sqrt{n}}{p^{5/8}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|w_{j}^{(p)}\right|>\frac{D(p)\sqrt{n}}{p^{5/8}}\right)\leq 2\sum_{j=1}^{p}e^{-\frac{nD^{2}(p)}{2p^{5/4}\sigma^{2}(p)}}

\displaystyle=2pe^{-\frac{nD^{2}(p)}{2p^{5/4}\sigma^{2}(p)}}\rightarrow 0

as
p\rightarrow\infty
, since
n=\widetilde{\Omega}\left(\frac{p^{c_{2}}\sigma^{2}(p)}{D^{2}(p)}\right)
and
c_{2}>\frac{5}{4}
. Hence, with probability at least
1-2pe^{-\frac{nD^{2}(p)}{2p^{5/4}\sigma^{2}(p)}}
, we have
\left\|\mathbb{W}^{(p)}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{D(p)\sqrt{n}}{p^{5/8}}
. Let
\Omega_{2}
be the event that the latter bound holds. Let
\Omega_{3}=\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}
, so
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{3})\geq 1-2pe^{\frac{-nC_{1}^{2}}{8p\left(C_{2}+C_{1}/3\right)}}-2pe^{-\frac{nD^{2}(p)}{2p^{5/4}\sigma^{2}(p)}}
.

Let us now work on
\Omega_{3}
. Note that
\beta_{\mathcal{L}}=\frac{\lambda_{\max}\left(\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}\right)}{n}\leq\frac{2C_{2}+C_{1}}{2}
. Fix
\theta\in\mathcal{C}
arbitrary. Since
||\theta^{*}||_{2}\geq(2S_{1}(2C_{2}/C_{1}+1)+1)D(p)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{\mathcal{C}}||\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})||_{2}}{\beta_{\mathcal{L}}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2||\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})||_{2}}{D(p)(2C_{2}+C_{1})}=\frac{2\left\|\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{Y}-\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}\theta\right\|_{2}}{D(p)(2C_{2}+C_{1})n}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\left\|\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}(\theta^{*}-\theta)-\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{W}^{(p)}\right\|_{2}}{D(p)(2C_{2}+C_{1})n}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}\right)(||\theta^{*}||_{2}-||\theta||_{2})}{D(p)(2C_{2}+C_{1})n}-\frac{2||\mathbb{X}/\sqrt{n}||_{2}\left\|\mathbb{W}^{(p)}\right\|_{2}}{D(p)(2C_{2}+C_{1})\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{4S_{1}\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}\right)}{C_{1}n}-\frac{2\sqrt{\beta_{\mathcal{L}}}\left\|\mathbb{W}^{(p)}\right\|_{2}}{D(p)(2C_{2}+C_{1})\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{4S_{1}\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbb{X}^{T}\mathbb{X}\right)}{C_{1}n}-\frac{\sqrt{2p}\left\|\mathbb{W}^{(p)}\right\|_{\infty}}{D(p)\sqrt{(2C_{2}+C_{1})n}},

since the
\ell_{2}
-norm is less than
\sqrt{p}
times the
\ell_{\infty}
-norm, and since
\beta_{\mathcal{L}}\leq\frac{2C_{2}+C_{1}}{2}
. Again, since we are on
\Omega_{3}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{\mathcal{C}}||\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})||_{2}}{\beta_{\mathcal{L}}}

\displaystyle\geq 2S_{1}-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{2C_{2}+C_{1}}}\frac{\sqrt{p}}{D(p)\sqrt{n}}\frac{D(p)\sqrt{n}}{p^{5/8}}=2S_{1}-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{2C_{2}+C_{1}}}\frac{1}{p^{1/8}}\geq S_{1},

as required, since
p\geq\left(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{S_{1}\sqrt{2C_{2}+C_{1}}}\right)^{8}
.

Finally, let us prove that the conditions (
1
) can be satisfied if
w^{(p)}
follows a
N(0,\sigma^{2}(p))
distribution truncated in the interval
[-1-\sqrt{p}K_{1}(p),1+\sqrt{p}K_{1}(p)]
. We then have
\mathbb{E}\left[w^{(p)}\right]=0
and
\left|w^{(p)}\right|\leq 1+\sqrt{p}K_{1}(p)
, with
w^{(p)}
having full support on
[-1-\sqrt{p}K_{1}(p),1+\sqrt{p}K_{1}(p)]
. By Theorem
2.1
in
[
6
]
, we know that
w^{(p)}
is sub-Gaussian with parameter

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}(p)\left(1-\frac{2(1+\sqrt{p}K_{1}(p))}{\sigma(p)}\frac{\phi\left(\frac{1+\sqrt{p}K_{1}(p)}{\sigma(p)}\right)}{2\Phi_{0}\left(\frac{1+\sqrt{p}K_{1}(p)}{\sigma(p)}\right)-1}\right),

which is less than
\sigma^{2}(p)
. Here,
\phi
and
\Phi_{0}
are the standard normal pdf and cdf, respectively. Hence,
w^{(p)}\in\mathcal{G}\left(\sigma^{2}(p)\right)
.

Remark 22
.

In Proposition
1
, we assumed that
C_{1}\leq\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)\leq\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma)\leq C_{2}\leq 1
. Observe that since
||x||_{\infty}\leq 1
, the variance of each entry in
x
is at most
\left(\frac{1+1}{2}\right)^{2}=1
, so the choice of
0<C_{1}\leq C_{2}\leq 1
ensures that the variance of each entry of
x
stays below
1
.

Remark 23
.

In Proposition
1
, we asked for

(2S_{1}(2C_{2}/C_{2}+1)+1)D(p)\leq||\theta^{*}||_{2}\leq K_{1}(p),

while in Theorem
2
, we optimize over
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{B}_{2}\left(D(p)\right)
. Thus, the lower bound on
||\theta^{*}||_{2}
scales as
D(p)
, even though the constants place
\theta^{*}
slightly outside
\mathcal{C}
. However, since
K_{1}(p),D(p)\rightarrow 0
, we have
||\theta_{T}-\theta^{*}||_{2}\lesssim D(p)+K_{1}(p)=O\left(\max\left\{D(p),K_{1}(p)\right\}\right)\rightarrow 0
as
p\rightarrow\infty
.

D.2.3
Proof of Proposition
2

For all
j\in[n]
, we have
y_{j}\in\{0,1\}
and
x_{j}\in\left\{-1,1\right\}^{p}
, so clearly,
|y_{j}|\leq 1
and
||x_{j}||_{\infty}\leq 1
. We now show that
\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{n}x_{j}x_{j}^{T}\right\|_{2}\leq wp(1+\tau)
. The matrix
X_{(-i)}
with the
x^{j}_{(-i)}
’s as rows has at least
(1-\tau)p
consensus columns, implying that

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{n}x_{j}x_{j}^{T}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{w}x^{j}_{(-i)}(x^{j}_{(-i)})^{T}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{k}z_{j}z_{j}^{T}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{w}\left\|x^{j}_{(-i)}\right\|_{2}^{2}+k||I_{p}||_{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{w}p+k=(1+\tau)wp,

as needed, where we used the facts that
x^{j}_{(-i)}
has all entries equal to either
-1
or
1
, and
Z^{T}Z=kI_{p}
. We now prove that
\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{n}y_{j}x_{j}\right\|_{2}\geq w\sqrt{(1-\tau)p}
. Since the target variables of the
z_{j}
’s are
0
and those of the
x^{j}_{(-i)}
’s are
1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{n}y_{j}x_{j}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{w}x^{j}_{(-i)}\right\|_{2}.

In the sum
\sum_{j=1}^{w}x^{j}_{(-i)}
, we have either
-w
or
w
in the positions of the consensus columns. Since
X_{(-i)}
has at least
(1-\tau)
consensus columns, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{n}y_{j}x_{j}\right\|_{2}\geq w\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{(1-\tau)p}1}=w\sqrt{(1-\tau)p}.

Now fix
\theta\in\mathcal{C}
. We have

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{\mathcal{C}}||\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})||_{2}}{\beta_{\mathcal{L}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{p}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{n}y_{j}x_{j}-x_{j}x_{j}^{T}\theta\right\|_{2}}{\alpha_{1}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{n}x_{j}x_{j}^{T}\right\|_{2}}\geq\frac{\sqrt{p}\left(w\sqrt{(1-\tau)p}-(1+\tau)wp||\theta||_{2}\right)}{\alpha_{1}(1+\tau)wp}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sqrt{p}\left(w\sqrt{(1-\tau)p}-(1+\tau)\alpha_{1}w\sqrt{p}\right)}{\alpha_{1}(1+\tau)wp}=\frac{\sqrt{1-\tau}-(1+\tau)\alpha_{1}}{\alpha_{1}(1+\tau)}\geq S_{1}.

Since
\theta\in\mathcal{C}
was arbitrary, we can take an infimum over all
\theta\in\mathcal{C}
to obtain
\mathop{\inf}\limits_{\theta\in\mathcal{C}}\frac{\alpha_{\mathcal{C}}||\nabla\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})||_{2}}{\beta_{\mathcal{L}}}\geq S_{1}
. Thus, the dataset in the hypothesis satisfies the inequalities (
6
), as required.

D.2.4
Proof of Proposition
3

Looking at the proof of Lemma
10
in
[
52
]
, they first obtain a result with high probability before passing to a result in expectation. Since in our setting,
p
and
||\theta^{*}||_{2}
are absolute constants, the curvature constant
\Gamma_{\mathcal{L}}
of
\mathcal{L}
and the Gaussian width
G_{\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})}
of
\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})
are also absolute constants. Hence, for
\zeta\in(0,1)
, the arguments in
[
52
]
imply that with probability at least
1-\zeta
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\theta_{T},\mathcal{D}_{n})-\mathcal{L}(\theta_{B,n},\mathcal{D}_{n})=\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{\log(T/\zeta)}{(n\epsilon)^{2/3}}\right)=\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{\log(n\epsilon/\zeta)}{(n\epsilon)^{2/3}}\right),

where
\theta_{B,n}\in\mathop{\arg\min}\limits_{\theta\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})}\mathcal{L}(\theta_{B,n},\mathcal{D}_{n})
and
\theta_{T}
is the output of Algorithm
2
. Denote this high-probability event by
\Omega_{7}
. In the proof of Theorem
6
, we showed the existence of an absolute constant
C_{1}>0
and an event
\Omega_{6}
, such that
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{6})\geq 1-\zeta
and
\mathcal{L}(\theta,\mathcal{D}_{n})
is
\frac{\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2})\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)}{2}
-strongly convex over
\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})
, for
n>C_{1}\log(2p/\zeta)
. Moreover, in the proof of Theorem
5
, we showed the existence of
C_{2},T_{\zeta},N_{\zeta}>0
and an event

\displaystyle\Omega_{5}=\left\{||\theta_{B,n}-\theta^{*}||_{2}\leq\frac{T_{\zeta}\log(n)}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}

such that
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{5})\geq 1-\zeta
, for
n\geq\max\left\{C_{2},N_{\zeta}\right\}
. Let
\Omega_{8}=\Omega_{5}\cap\Omega_{6}\cap\Omega_{7}
, so
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{8})\geq 1-3\zeta
. On the event
\Omega_{8}
, for
n>\max\left\{C_{1}\log(2p/\zeta),C_{2},N_{\zeta}\right\}
, we see that since
\frac{\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{x}||\theta^{*}||_{2})\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)}{2}\asymp 1
and
\theta_{B,n}
is a minimizer over
\mathbb{B}_{2}(||\theta^{*}||_{2})
, strong convexity implies that

\displaystyle||\theta_{T}-\theta_{B,n}||_{2}=\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{\log^{1/2}(n\epsilon/\zeta)}{(n\epsilon)^{1/3}}\right).

Using the triangle inequality, we then have

\displaystyle||\theta_{T}-\theta^{*}||_{2}\leq||\theta_{T}-\theta_{B,n}||_{2}+||\theta_{B,n}-\theta^{*}||_{2}=\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{T_{\zeta}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\log^{1/2}(n\epsilon/\zeta)}{(n\epsilon)^{1/3}}\right),

as required.

D.2.5
Gradient bound for heavy-tailed data

We now state and prove the main result about gradient estimators used in Algorithm
4
. We provide a proof since we aim to correct the aspect related to the choice of
b
in
[
46
]
, as discussed in Section
3.3
.

Lemma 29
.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a generic loss. Suppose
\mathcal{D}_{n}=\{z_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are i.i.d. samples from a heavy-tailed distribution. Then Algorithm
4
, with
